       1 +# Brief for prose-writing subagents (Track B, M2)              
       2 +                                                               
       3 +You are writing the synthetic email/notes corpus for a triage-t
         +ool evaluation. The world is fictional. Read, in order:

         +le).                                                           
       8 +                                                               
       9 +Hard rules                                                     
      10 +- **Never open** `digest/`, `prompts/`, `runs/`, `eval/`, `test
         +s/`. You only read `world/dev/**` and write under `world/dev/pr
         +ose/**`.                                                       
      11 +- Every `must_include` phrase listed for a beat (storylines) or
         + an item (background.yaml) appears **verbatim** in that message
         +'s `body` (same case, same punctuation, same currency symbols).
         + Check each one before you finish.                             
      12 +- Never write the words: trap, P0, P1, P2, P3, storyline, expec
         +ted, "must include", answer key, eval, label. Never hint that s
         +omething is a test.

         +rs for either of them, anywhere (other people may be gendered f
         +reely).

         +ignature: default` appends the world.yaml signature; use `signa
         +ture: none` for Avery's short replies and for casual/internal o
         +ne-liners; use a literal string for a deliberately long or diff
         +erent signature.                                               
      15 +- Realistic, varied lengths (2 lines to 30 lines). About 30% of
         + human mail carries a hardness knob: a typo, an indirect phrasi
         +ng ("I'll get that over to you" instead of "I will send X by Y"
         +), two topics in one email, a P.S., an over-long signature, or 
         +a quoted history (`quotes_previous: true` on replies).         
      16 +- Replies in a thread set `reply_to` to the local id they answe
         +r and usually `quotes_previous: true`. Subjects: only the first
         + message's subject is written; replies get "Re: " from the rend
         +erer.

         +y message (or the message is from Avery), except messages insid
         +e a `forward_of` chain.                                        
      18 +- Dates: `day` 1..30 (see FORMAT.md for the weekday table); wee
         +kend days have far less mail; business hours mostly, with some 
         +evening mail from Avery, Sam, Jordan, Tomás.                   
      19 +- Don't invent people with the same names as world.yaml people.

         + domain (never a real company's brand as the sender org unless 
         +it is a well-known SaaS tool or newsletter named in background.
         +yaml).

       1 +"""Relative days → PT datetimes for a given anchor (date of day
         + 30). DST-correct via zoneinfo."""                             
       2 +from __future__ import annotations                             
       3 +                                                               
       4 +from datetime import date, datetime, time, timedelta           
       5 +from email.utils import format_datetime                        
       6 +from zoneinfo import ZoneInfo                                  
       7 +                                                               
       8 +TZ = ZoneInfo("America/Los_Angeles")                           
       9 +UTC = ZoneInfo("UTC")                                          
      10 +                                                               
      11 +                                                               
      12 +class Timeline:                                                
      13 +    def __init__(self, anchor: date, days: int = 30):          
      14 +        self.anchor = anchor                                   
      15 +        self.days = days                                       
      16 +                                                               
      17 +    def date(self, day: int) -> date:                          
      18 +        return self.anchor - timedelta(days=self.days - day)   
      19 +                                                               
      20 +    def day_of(self, d: date) -> int:                          
      21 +        return self.days - (self.anchor - d).days              
      22 +                                                               
      23 +    def dt(self, day: int, hhmm: str, second: int = 0) -> datet
         +ime:                                                           
      24 +        h, m = (int(x) for x in hhmm.split(":"))               
      25 +        return datetime.combine(self.date(day), time(h, m, seco
         +nd), tzinfo=TZ)                                                
      26 +                                                               
      27 +    def rfc2822(self, when: datetime) -> str:                  
      28 +        return format_datetime(when)                           
      29 +                                                               
      30 +    @staticmethod                                              
      31 +    def ics_utc(when: datetime) -> str:                        
      32 +        return when.astimezone(UTC).strftime("%Y%m%dT%H%M%SZ") 
      33 +                                                               
      34 +    @staticmethod                                              
      35 +    def ics_local(when: datetime) -> str:                      
      36 +        return when.strftime("%Y%m%dT%H%M%S")                  
      37 +                                                               
      38 +    def in_window(self, when: datetime, before_days: int = 0, a
         +fter_days: int = 0) -> bool:                                   
      39 +        lo = datetime.combine(self.date(1) - timedelta(days=bef
         +ore_days), time(0, 0), tzinfo=TZ)                              
      40 +        hi = datetime.combine(self.date(self.days) + timedelta(
         +days=after_days), time(23, 59, 59), tzinfo=TZ)

     -margin-squeeze.eml (+32 -0)
       1 +Message-ID: <20260826-0612.email@stratechery.com>              
       2 +Date: Wed, 26 Aug 2026 06:12:44 -0700                          
       3 +From: Stratechery <email@stratechery.com>                      
       4 +To: Avery Chen <avery@tessera.io>                              
       5 +Subject: Stratechery: The Inference Margin Squeeze             
       6 +List-Unsubscribe: <mailto:unsubscribe@stratechery.com?subject=u
         +nsubscribe>, <https://stratechery.com/u/5de20878>              
       7 +Precedence: bulk                                               
       8 +Content-Type: text/plain; charset="utf-8"                      
       9 +Content-Transfer-Encoding: 8bit                                
      10 +MIME-Version: 1.0                                              
      11 +                                                               
      12 +STRATECHERY                                                    
      13 +Wednesday, August 26, 2026                                     
      14 +                                                               
      15 +The Inference Margin Squeeze                                   
      16 +                                                               
      17 +There is a pattern in every platform shift that I keep coming b
         +ack to: the layer that looks like the business ends up being th
         +e cost center, and the layer that looks like plumbing ends up b
         +eing the business. In the PC era the hardware was the product u
         +ntil it wasn't; Microsoft and Intel captured the profit while t
         +he box-makers fought over single-digit margins. The question fo
         +r the current cycle is which layer is the box-maker.           
      18 +                                                               
      19 +My growing suspicion is that it is the application layer, at le
         +ast as currently constituted. Consider the unit economics of a 
         +typical "AI-native" vertical SaaS company. It sells a seat, or 
         +increasingly a workflow, for a fixed monthly price. Underneath 
         +that price sits a variable cost: tokens, and those tokens are p
         +riced by someone else. When a customer's usage doubles because 
         +the product worked, the vendor's revenue stays flat and its cos
         +t of goods doubles. That is the exact inverse of the software b
         +usiness model we have spent twenty-five years celebrating.     
      20 +                                                               
      21 +The obvious response is usage-based pricing, and many companies
         + are moving there. But usage pricing trades one problem for ano
         +ther: it makes the vendor's margin transparent, and transparent
         + margins invite the customer to ask why they are paying a marku
         +p on tokens they could buy directly. The vendor's answer has to
         + be "because of what we do with them," which is a real answer, 
         +but it is a services answer, not a software answer.            
      22 +                                                               
      23 +The second response is to push the cost down the stack: negotia
         +te volume discounts, route to cheaper models, cache aggressivel
         +y, distill. This works, and it is where most of the actual engi
         +neering effort in the industry is going right now. It also mean
         +s the application company's most important supplier relationshi
         +p is with its inference provider, which is a strange place for 
         +a software company to find itself.                             
      24 +                                                               
      25 +The third response, and the one I find most interesting, is to 
         +change the customer. Companies that sell outcomes rather than s
         +oftware (a resolved ticket, a reconciled invoice, a completed o
         +nboarding) can absorb variable cost because they are pricing th
         +e value, not the seat. The catch is that outcome pricing requir
         +es the product to actually work, reliably, without a human in t
         +he loop, which is a much higher bar than "helpful."            
      26 +                                                               
      27 +I don't think any of this is fatal to the application layer. I 
         +do think the companies that survive will look less like the Saa
         +S playbook and more like something new: part software, part man
         +aged service, with gross margins that would have made a 2019 in
         +vestor wince. The squeeze is real; the question is who gets squ
         +eezed.                                                         
      28 +                                                               
      29 +Read on the web: https://stratechery.com/2026/the-inference-mar
         +gin-squeeze/                                                   
      30 +                                                               
      31 +—                                                              
      32 +You are receiving this because you subscribed to Stratechery Da
         +ily Updates. Manage your subscription or unsubscribe: https://s
         +tratechery.com/account/

         +tup and an off-the-shelf defect model retrained on about 2,000

         +he same view the model saw. When the model flagged a skewed pan

         +n top. The wrapping camp is winning the argument industry-wide

  What was built

  - Data: digest generate --world dev renders data/dev/ with 523 .eml, both
    .ics, 10 notes and tasks.md, plus data/dev__fulfilled/ for the S1 variant,
    and writes the answer key eval/manifests/dev.yaml. It takes about two
    seconds and is reproducible from world files + prose + seed.
  - Prose: ~160 thread files, 5 bulk files and 10 notes under world/dev/prose/,
    written by 11 parallel subagents against FORMAT.md
    (world/dev/prose/FORMAT.md). Every storyline beat, planted pattern and
    classification case is present with its must_include phrases verbatim.
  - Generator code in generator/: world loader, PT timeline, RFC 5322 renderer
    (RFC 2047 headers, readable 8-bit bodies, quoted history, Gmail-style nested
    forwards for the 14-message Northstar chain), ICS renderer (VTIMEZONE,
    RRULE, PARTSTAT, CREATED), notes/tasks renderer with the last-modified
    header A asked for, manifest emitter, and the §9 validator.
  - Validator passes on the real build: threading resolves, PT offsets correct,
    no label words, pronoun check, category counts inside ±10%. It refuses to
    write the manifest otherwise.
